\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}=\big\{D=\{D(\omega):\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}\}:D\text{ satisfying }\eqref{D_1}\big\}.

Define

(6.7)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}(t,\omega)=\boldsymbol{u}(t,\omega)-\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\ \text{ and }\ \boldsymbol{v}_{m}(t,\omega)=\boldsymbol{u}_{m}(t,\omega)-\textbf{g}_{m}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega).

Then, from (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), we obtain pathwise deterministic systems (on
\mathcal{O}
)

(6.8)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/\boldsymbol{v}}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}&=\mu\Delta\boldsymbol{v}-\big((\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y})\cdot\nabla\big)(\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{g}\boldsymbol{y})-\alpha\boldsymbol{v}-\beta|\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y}|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y})\\
&\qquad-\nabla p+\boldsymbol{f}+(\ell-\alpha)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}\boldsymbol{y}+\mu\boldsymbol{y}\Delta\mathrm{\textbf{g}},\text{ in }\ \mathcal{O}\times(0,\infty),\\
\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}&=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \text{ in }\ \mathcal{O}\times(0,\infty),\\
\boldsymbol{v}&=\boldsymbol{0},\ \qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{ on }\ \partial\mathcal{O}\times(0,\infty),\\
\boldsymbol{v}(0)&=\boldsymbol{x}-\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y}(\omega),\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \text{ in }\ \mathcal{O},\end{aligned}}}\right.

and (on
\mathcal{O}_{m}
,
m\in\bar{\mathbb{N}}
)

(6.9)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/\boldsymbol{v}_{m}}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}&=\mu\Delta\boldsymbol{v}_{m}-\big((\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}_{m}\boldsymbol{y})\cdot\nabla\big)(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\mathrm{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y})-\alpha\boldsymbol{v}_{m}-\beta|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}_{m}\boldsymbol{y})\\
&\qquad-\nabla p_{m}+\boldsymbol{f}_{m}+(\ell-\alpha)\boldsymbol{\mathrm{g}}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}+\mu\boldsymbol{y}\Delta\mathrm{\textbf{g}}_{m},\text{ in }\ \mathcal{O}_{m}\times(0,\infty),\\
\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{m}&=0,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\ \text{ in }\ \mathcal{O}_{m}\times(0,\infty),\\
\boldsymbol{v}_{m}&=\boldsymbol{0},\ \qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\text{ on }\ \partial\mathcal{O}_{m}\times(0,\infty),\\
\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0)&=\boldsymbol{x}_{m}-\mathrm{\textbf{g}}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\omega),\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\ \text{ in }\ \mathcal{O}_{m},\end{aligned}}}\right.

respectively. Let us define the space (for
m\in\bar{\mathbb{N}}
)

\mathcal{V}_{m}:=\{\boldsymbol{u}_{m}\in\mathrm{C}_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{m},\mathbb{R}^{2}):\nabla\cdot\boldsymbol{u}_{m}=0\},

where
\mathrm{C}_{0}^{\infty}(\mathcal{O}_{m};\mathbb{R}^{2})
denote the space of all infinitely differentiable functions (
\mathbb{R}^{2}
-valued) with compact support in
\mathcal{O}_{m}
. Let
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
,
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
, for
p\in(2,\infty)
denote the completion of
\mathcal{V}_{m}
in
\mathrm{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{m};\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
\mathrm{H}_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{m};\mathbb{R}^{2})
and
\mathrm{L}^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{m};\mathbb{R}^{2})
norms respectively. The spaces
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
,
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
are endowed with the norms
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}^{2}:=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}}|\boldsymbol{u}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x
,
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}^{2}:=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{u}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x
and
\|\boldsymbol{u}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}^{2}:=\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}}|\boldsymbol{u}(x)|^{p}\/\mathrm{d}\/x,
respectively. The induced duality between the spaces
\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
and
\mathbb{V}^{\prime}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
, and
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
and its dual
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{p}{p-1}}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
is denoted by
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle.

For all
t\geq 0,
and for every
\boldsymbol{v}(0)\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
and
\omega\in\Omega
, (
6.9
) has a unique solution in
\mathrm{C}\big([0,T];\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})\big)\cap\mathrm{L}^{2}\big(0,T;\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})\big)\cap\mathrm{L}^{r+1}\big(0,T;\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})\big)
, for all
m\in\bar{\mathbb{N}}
. Define a cocycle
\Phi_{m}:\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})\to\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
for the system (
6.2
) such that for given
\omega\in\Omega
,
\boldsymbol{u}(s)\in\mathbb{H}
and for all
t\geq s
,

(6.10)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{m}(t,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{u}_{m}(s))=\Phi_{m}(t-s;\theta_{s}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{m}(s)=\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(t,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(s))+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega).

For the existence of a unique random attractor of the system (
6.2
) in
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
, we refer the readers to
[
40
]
. See section 5 in
[
40
]
for unbounded domains, and for bounded domains see section 3 in
[
40
]
and the work
[
39
]
, where authors have used compact Sobolev embedding to prove their results in bounded domains. In this work, we are proving upper semicontinuity of random attractor for 2D SCBF equations when
r>1
(for
r=1
, see Remark
6.10
below).

Definition 6.1

(Expansion and restriction of a function)
.

For a function
\boldsymbol{u}:\mathcal{O}_{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{2},
its null-expansion
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}:\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
is defined by

(6.11)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}(x)=\begin{cases}\boldsymbol{u}(x),\ &x\in\mathcal{O}_{m},\\
\boldsymbol{0},\ &x\in\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{m}.\end{cases}

For a function
\boldsymbol{v}:\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{R}^{2},
the restriction
\boldsymbol{v}|_{\mathcal{O}_{m}}:\mathcal{O}_{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
is given by

\boldsymbol{v}|_{\mathcal{O}_{m}}(x)=\boldsymbol{v}(x),\quad x\in\mathcal{O}_{m}.

On taking the orthogonal projections
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathcal{O}_{m}}:\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{m})\to\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
(see subsection
2.2
) onto the equations (
6.8
) and (
6.9
), respectively, we obtain following systems on
\mathcal{O}
and
\mathcal{O}_{m}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
):

(6.12)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/\boldsymbol{v}}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}&=-\mu\mathrm{A}\boldsymbol{v}(t)-\mathrm{B}(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})-\alpha\boldsymbol{v}-\beta\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})\\
&\quad+\boldsymbol{f}+(\ell-\alpha)\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}+\mu\boldsymbol{y}\Delta\textbf{g},\\
\boldsymbol{v}(0)&=\boldsymbol{x}-\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\omega),\end{aligned}}}\right.

and

(6.13)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/\boldsymbol{v}_{m}}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}&=-\mu\mathrm{A}_{m}\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(t)-\mathrm{B}_{m}(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y})-\alpha\boldsymbol{v}_{m}-\beta\mathcal{C}_{m}(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y})\\
&\quad+\boldsymbol{f}_{m}+(\ell-\alpha)\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}+\mu\boldsymbol{y}\Delta\textbf{g}_{m},\\
\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0)&=\boldsymbol{x}_{m}-\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\omega),\end{aligned}}}\right.

respectively, where
\mathrm{A}_{m},\mathrm{B}_{m}
and
\mathcal{C}_{m}
are defined on
\mathcal{O}_{m}
in a similar way as of
\mathrm{A},\mathrm{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
defined on
\mathcal{O}
in section
2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
represent the random attractor for the 2D SCBF equations (
6.2
) in
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
for
m\in\bar{\mathbb{N}}
. In this section, we prove the upper semicontinuity from
\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
to
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)
as
m\to\infty
, that is, as the bounded domain
\mathcal{O}_{m}
is expanded to the unbounded domain
\mathcal{O}.
The main difficulty is the proper definition of the Hausdorff semidistance between
\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) and
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)
, since they lie in different spaces
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) and
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
. In order to overcome the above difficulty, we consider the null-expansion
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}
of solution
\boldsymbol{u}_{m}
defined by

(6.14)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}=\begin{cases}\boldsymbol{u}_{m},\ &x\in\mathcal{O}_{m},\\
\boldsymbol{0},\ &x\in\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{m}.\end{cases}

It can be easily seen that
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
if
\boldsymbol{u}_{m}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
. Notice that
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) is not equal to
\boldsymbol{u}_{\infty}.
Hence, the null-expansion of random attractor
\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
is denoted by
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m}(\omega)
and is define as follows:

\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m}(\omega)=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}):\text{there exists }\ \boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega),\text{ such that }\ \boldsymbol{u}=\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}\},\text{ for all }\omega\in\Omega.

It follows that all
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m}(\omega)
lies in the same space
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}),
and thus, the Hausdorff semidistance can be understood in the following sense:

(6.15)

\displaystyle d_{m}(\omega):=\text{dist}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m}(\omega),\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)\big)=\sup_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m}(\omega)}\inf_{\boldsymbol{v}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)}\|\boldsymbol{u}-\boldsymbol{v}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}.

Hence, our aim in this section is to prove
d_{m}(\omega)\to 0
as
m\to\infty.
In the sequel, we use the tilde symbol
\ \widetilde{\cdot}\
to denote the null-expansion of a function, a set, an operator, etc.

6.1.
Random Dynamical systems and their expansions for SCBF equations

We will assume
m\in\mathbb{N}
for this section unless stated. Let
\Phi_{m}(t,\omega):\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})\to\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
be the RDS generated by 2D SCBF equations (
6.2
) for domain
\mathcal{O}_{m}
and a natural expansion of
\Phi_{m}
is given by
\widetilde{\Phi}_{m}:\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})\to\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
,

(6.16)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\Phi}_{m}\boldsymbol{u})(x)=\begin{cases}(\Phi_{m}(\boldsymbol{u}|_{\mathcal{O}_{m}}))(x),\ &\text{ for all }x\in\mathcal{O}_{m},\\
0,\ &\text{ for all }x\in\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{m}.\end{cases}

The following Lemma is useful to obtain the absorbing set.

Lemma 6.2
.

For
r>1
, let
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
and
\textbf{g}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})
. Then for all
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
and for every
D=\{D(\omega):\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}\}\in\mathfrak{D}
, there exists
\mathcal{T}_{\infty}=\mathcal{T}_{\infty}(\omega,D)>0
such that for all
t\geq\mathcal{T}_{\infty}
, the solution
\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot)
of (
6.12
) satisfies

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}(0,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\mu\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\ \/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle+\beta\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\leq M\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\|\textbf{g}\|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})}

(6.17)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta=:\mathscr{L}(\omega),

and

(6.18)

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[-t,0]}\|\boldsymbol{v}(s,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\mathscr{L}(\omega).

where
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in D(\theta_{-t}\omega)
and
M>0
is a constant independent of
\omega
and
D
.

Proof.

Taking the inner product with
\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot)
to the first equation in (
6.12
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}=

\displaystyle-\mu\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}-\alpha\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}-b\big(\boldsymbol{v}(t)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(t)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big)

\displaystyle-\beta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}),\boldsymbol{v}(t)}}\right\rangle+\big(\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big)+(\ell-\alpha)\big(\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big)

\displaystyle-\big(\mu\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\mathrm{A}\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mu\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}-\alpha\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}-b\big(\boldsymbol{v}(t)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big)

\displaystyle-\beta\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}+\beta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{v}(t)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}),\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}}}\right\rangle

(6.19)

\displaystyle+\big(\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big)+(\ell-\alpha)\big(\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big)-\big(\mu\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\mathrm{A}\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(t)\big),

for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
, where we have used (
2.2
). Applying Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities, we obtain for
r>1

\displaystyle\big|(\ell-\alpha)\big(\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}\big)\big|+\big|\big(\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{v}\big)\big|+\big|\big(\mu\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\mathrm{A}\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}\big)\big|

(6.20)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})}}}\right]|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2},

\displaystyle\big|b\big(\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)}}\right|\|\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\|\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})}

(6.21)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}+\frac{\mu}{2}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\|\textbf{g}\|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})},

and

\displaystyle\beta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}(\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}),\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\beta|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)|\|\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\|\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}

(6.22)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{4}\|\boldsymbol{v}+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}+C|\boldsymbol{z}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}.

Combining (
6.1
)-(
6.1
) and using it in (
6.1
), we find

\displaystyle\frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\mu\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\alpha\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\beta\|\boldsymbol{v}(t)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})}}}\right]|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{2}+C\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{t}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\|\textbf{g}\|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\quad+C|\boldsymbol{z}(\theta_{t}\omega)|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}.

for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
. By means of variation of constants formula, we obtain

\displaystyle e^{\alpha t_{1}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t_{1})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\mu\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\beta\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta)+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha t_{2}}\|\boldsymbol{v}(t_{2})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+C\int_{t_{2}}^{t_{1}}e^{\alpha\zeta}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\|\textbf{g}\|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})}+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta.

Therefore, for any
t>0
,
s\geq-t
(for
s\in[-t,0]
) and
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in D(\theta_{-t}\omega)
, we get

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}(s,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\mu\int_{-t}^{s}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\ \/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle+\beta\int_{-t}^{s}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha t}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+C\int_{-t}^{s}e^{\alpha\zeta}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\|\textbf{g}\|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})}+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha t}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+C\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}

(6.23)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\|\textbf{g}\|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})}+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta.

Since,
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in D(\theta_{-t}\omega)
and
D\in\mathfrak{D}
, we have

(6.24)

\displaystyle e^{-\alpha t}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\leq e^{-\alpha t}\|D(\theta_{-t}\omega)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\to 0\text{ as }t\to\infty.

From (
6.4
), we infer that the second term on the right hand side of inequality (
6.1
) is finite. Moreover, (
6.24
) implies that there exists
\mathcal{T}_{\infty}=\mathcal{T}_{\infty}(\omega,D)
such that

\displaystyle e^{-\alpha t}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{f}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\|\textbf{g}\|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}(\mathcal{O})}+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta,\ \ \text{ for all }t\geq\mathcal{T}_{\infty},

which gives (
6.2
) along with (
6.1
) and (
6.24
) for
s=0,
and (
6.18
) is immediate.
∎

The following Lemma can be proved in a similar way as Lemma
6.2
.

Lemma 6.3
.

For
r>1
, let
\boldsymbol{f}_{m}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
and
\textbf{g}_{m}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A}_{m})
. Then for all
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
and for every
D=\{D(\omega):\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}\}\in\mathfrak{D}
, there exists
\mathcal{T}_{m}=\mathcal{T}_{m}(\omega,D)>0
such that for all
t\geq\mathcal{T}_{m}
, the solution
\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\cdot)
of (
6.13
) satisfies

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+\mu\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\ \/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

(6.25)

\displaystyle+\beta\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)\textbf{g}_{m}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\leq\mathscr{L}(\omega),

and

(6.26)

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[-t,0]}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(s,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq\mathscr{L}(\omega),

where
\mathscr{L}(\omega)
is same as in (
6.2
) and
\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0}\in D(\theta_{-t}\omega)
.

It implies from Lemmas
6.2
and
6.3
that, for every
D=\{D(\omega):\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}\}
and
\mathbb{P}
-a.e.
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
, there exists a time
\mathcal{T}(\omega,D)=\max\limits_{m\in\bar{\mathbb{N}}}\mathcal{T}_{m}(\omega,D)>0
such that for all
t\geq\mathcal{T}(\omega,D)
,

(6.27)

\displaystyle\|\Phi_{m}(t,\theta_{-t}\omega,D(\theta_{-t}\omega))\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq[\mathscr{L}^{*}(\omega)]^{2},

where
[\mathscr{L}^{*}(\omega)]^{2}=\mathscr{L}(\omega)+\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\omega)}}\right|^{2}
and
\mathscr{L}(\omega)
is the same as in (
6.2
), that is, there exists a random
\mathfrak{D}
-absorbing set
\mathcal{K}_{m}(\omega)
of
\Phi_{m}
(
m\in\bar{\mathbb{N}}
) given by

(6.28)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{m}(\omega):=\{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m}):\|\boldsymbol{u}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq[\mathscr{L}^{*}(\omega)]^{2}\}.

Lemma 6.4
.

For
r>1
, assume that
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
,
\textbf{g}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})
. Then, for any
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in D(\theta_{-t}\omega),
where
D=\{D(\omega):\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}\}\in\mathfrak{D}
, and for any
\varepsilon>0
and
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
, there exists
\mathscr{T}_{\infty}=\mathscr{T}_{\infty}(\omega,D,\varepsilon)\geq 0
and
K_{1}(\omega,\varepsilon)>0
such that for all
t\geq\mathscr{T}_{\infty}
and
k\geq K_{1}
, the solution of (
6.12
) satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}(0,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\mu\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

(6.29)

\displaystyle+\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\leq\varepsilon,

and

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[-t,0]}\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}(s,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

Let
\uprho
be a smooth function such that
0\leq\uprho(s)\leq 1
for
s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
and

(6.30)

\displaystyle\uprho(s)=\begin{cases}0,\text{ for }0\leq s\leq 1,\\
1,\text{ for }s\geq 2.\end{cases}

Then, there exists a positive constant
C
such that
|\uprho^{\prime}(s)|\leq C
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Taking divergence to the first equation of (
6.8
), formally we obtain

\displaystyle-\Delta p

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\big((\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y})\cdot\nabla\big)(\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{g}\boldsymbol{y})}}\right]+\beta\nabla\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y}|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\nabla\cdot\big((\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})\otimes(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})\big)}}\right]+\beta\nabla\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y}|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y})}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\big((v_{i}+\text{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{y})(v_{j}+\text{g}_{j}\boldsymbol{y})\big)+\beta\nabla\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y}|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y})}}\right],

which implies that

(6.31)

\displaystyle p=(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x_{i}\partial x_{j}}\big((v_{i}+\text{g}_{i}\boldsymbol{y})(v_{j}+\text{g}_{j}\boldsymbol{y})\big)+\beta\nabla\cdot\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[|\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y}|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\mathrm{\textbf{g}}\boldsymbol{y})}}\right]}}\right].

Taking the inner product to the first equation of (
6.8
) with
\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle=\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\Delta\boldsymbol{v})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x-\alpha\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x-b\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\beta\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x-\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\nabla p)\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\int_{\mathcal{O}}\boldsymbol{f}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\quad+(\ell-\alpha)\boldsymbol{y}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\textbf{g}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\mu\boldsymbol{y}\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\Delta\textbf{g})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle=\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\Delta\boldsymbol{v})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x-\alpha\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x-b\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+b\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right)-\beta\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\/\mathrm{d}\/x-\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\nabla p)\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\int_{\mathcal{O}}\boldsymbol{f}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

(6.32)

\displaystyle\quad+(\ell-\alpha)\boldsymbol{y}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\textbf{g}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\mu\boldsymbol{y}\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\Delta\textbf{g})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x.

We estimate each term on right hand side of (
6.1
). Integration by parts and divergence free condition of
\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot)
help us to obtain

\displaystyle\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\Delta\boldsymbol{v})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle=-\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/x-\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\frac{2}{k^{2}}(x\cdot\nabla)\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle=-\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/x-\mu\int\limits_{k\leq|x|\leq\sqrt{2}k}\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\frac{2}{k^{2}}(x\cdot\nabla)\boldsymbol{v}\cdot\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq-\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\frac{2\sqrt{2}\mu}{k}\int\limits_{k\leq|x|\leq\sqrt{2}k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}}\right|\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq-\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\frac{C}{k}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}}}\right|\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq-\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\frac{C}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right)

(6.33)

\displaystyle\leq-\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\frac{C}{k}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\ b\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\textbf{g},\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\textbf{g}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\frac{x}{k^{2}}\cdot(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})|\textbf{g}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int\limits_{k\leq|x|\leq\sqrt{2}k}\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\frac{x}{k^{2}}\cdot(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})|\textbf{g}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\frac{\sqrt{2}}{k}\int\limits_{k\leq|x|\leq\sqrt{2}k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)}}\right||\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}||\textbf{g}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\frac{C}{k}\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right]

(6.34)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right],

and

\displaystyle-b\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\frac{x}{k^{2}}\cdot(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{k\leq|x|\leq\sqrt{2}k}\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\frac{x}{k^{2}}\cdot(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{k}\int\limits_{k\leq|x|\leq\sqrt{2}k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)}}\right||\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}|^{3}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{3}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{3}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\frac{C}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right]

(6.35)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right],

where we have used interpolation and Young’s inequalities in the last inequality. Using integration by parts, divergence free condition and (
6.31
), we obtain

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\nabla p)\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{O}}p\uprho^{\prime}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\frac{2}{k^{2}}(x\cdot\boldsymbol{v})\leq\frac{C}{k}\int\limits_{k\leq|x|\leq\sqrt{2}k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|p}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}}}\right|\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{2}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{r}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]

(6.36)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{r+1}\|\boldsymbol{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg].

Finally, we estimate the remaining terms of (
6.1
) by using Hölder’s and Young’s inequalities as follows, for
r>1
,

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{y}b\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{v},\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\textbf{g}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\boldsymbol{y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x}}\right]^{\frac{1}{r+1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/x}}\right]^{\frac{r-1}{2(r+1)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\frac{\mu}{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

(6.37)

\displaystyle\quad+C\boldsymbol{y}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/x,

\displaystyle\beta\boldsymbol{y}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r-1}(\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\textbf{g}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

(6.38)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta}{4}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{r+1}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\textbf{g}(x)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}\boldsymbol{f}(x)\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

(6.39)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\frac{1}{\alpha}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{f}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x,

\displaystyle(\ell-\alpha)\boldsymbol{y}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\textbf{g}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\mu\boldsymbol{y}\int_{\mathcal{O}}(\Delta\textbf{g})\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\boldsymbol{v}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

(6.40)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha}{4}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\Delta\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x.

Combining (
6.1
)-(
6.40
), we get

\displaystyle\frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\alpha\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle+\mu\int_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}|^{2}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\beta\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]+C\boldsymbol{y}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{r+1}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\textbf{g}(x)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\boldsymbol{f}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\uprho\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{|x|^{2}}{k^{2}}}}\right)|\Delta\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\boldsymbol{y}^{4}\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}}}\right|^{r+1}\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg]+C\boldsymbol{y}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\int_{|x|\geq k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{r+1}\int_{|x|\geq k}|\textbf{g}(x)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x

(6.41)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{|x|\geq k}|\boldsymbol{f}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int_{|x|\geq k}|\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+C\boldsymbol{y}^{2}\int_{|x|\geq k}|\Delta\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x.

Applying variation of constant formula, for
s\in[-t,0]
, we find

\displaystyle\int_{|x|\geq k}|\boldsymbol{v}(s,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\mu\int_{-t}^{s}e^{\alpha\zeta}\int_{|x|\geq k}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle+\int_{-t}^{s}e^{\alpha\zeta}\int_{|x|\geq k}|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\alpha t}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}+\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\sup_{\zeta\in[-t,0]}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)\boldsymbol{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{4}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\bigg]+C\bigg[\int_{|x|\geq k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\textbf{g}(x)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\boldsymbol{f}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\Delta\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\bigg].

Since
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\in D(\theta_{-t}\omega)
, and
D\in\mathfrak{D}
, from (
6.24
), we infer that, for given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\mathscr{T}_{1}=\mathscr{T}_{1}(\omega,D,\varepsilon)>0
such that for all
t\geq\mathscr{T}_{1}
,

\displaystyle e^{-\alpha t}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{3}.

Due to Lemma
6.2
, we conclude that, for given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
k_{1}=k_{1}(\omega,\varepsilon)>0
and
\mathscr{T}_{2}=\mathscr{T}_{2}(\omega,D,\varepsilon)>0
such that for all
k\geq k_{1}
and
t\geq\mathscr{T}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\sup_{\zeta\in[-t,0]}\|\boldsymbol{v}(0,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})+\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)\boldsymbol{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\bigg]\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{3}.

Since,
\textbf{g}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})
,
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
and by (
6.4
), we get

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\bigg[1+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{4}+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}+|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\bigg]\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta<\infty,

and that for given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
k_{2}=k_{2}(\omega,\varepsilon)>0
such that for all
k\geq k_{2}
,

\displaystyle\frac{C}{k}\bigg[\|\textbf{g}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\|\textbf{g}\|^{4}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{4}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{4}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\|\textbf{g}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\bigg]+C\bigg[\int_{|x|\geq k}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{r-1}}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\textbf{g}(x)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\boldsymbol{f}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta+\int_{|x|\geq k}|\Delta\textbf{g}(x)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\bigg]\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{3}.

Let us take
\mathscr{T}_{\infty}=\mathscr{T}_{\infty}(\omega,D,\varepsilon)=\max\{\mathscr{T}_{1},\mathscr{T}_{2}\}
and
K_{1}(\omega,\varepsilon)=\max\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
, so that the proof can be completed.
∎

The following Lemma can be proved in a similar way as Lemma
6.4
.

Lemma 6.5
.

For
r>1
, assume that
\boldsymbol{f}_{m}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})
,
\textbf{g}_{m}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A}_{m})
. Then, for any
\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0}\in D(\theta_{-t}\omega),
where
D=\{D(\omega):\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}\}\in\mathfrak{D}
, and for any
\varepsilon>0
and
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
, there exists
\mathscr{T}_{m}=\mathscr{T}_{m}(\omega,D,\varepsilon)\geq 0
and
K_{2}(\omega,\varepsilon)>0
such that for all
t\geq\mathscr{T}_{m}
and
m\geq k\geq K_{2}
, the solution of (
6.13
) satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x+\mu\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta

(6.42)

\displaystyle+\int_{-t}^{0}e^{\alpha\zeta}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\zeta,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\zeta\leq\varepsilon,

and

(6.43)

\displaystyle\sup_{s\in[-t,0]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(s,-t;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x}}\right]\leq\varepsilon.

Lemma 6.6
.

Assume that
\boldsymbol{f}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
. Let
\boldsymbol{v}_{m}
and
\boldsymbol{v}
be the solutions of (
6.13
) and (
6.12
) with initial data
\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0}
and
\boldsymbol{v}_{0}
, respectively. If
\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}_{0}
in
\mathbb{H}
as
n\to\infty
, then for every
\omega\in\tilde{\Omega}
,

(i)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\xi,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})
in
\mathbb{H}
for all
\xi\geq s
.

(ii)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\cdot,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})
in
\mathrm{L}^{2}((s,s+T);\mathbb{V})
for every
T>0
.

(iii)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\cdot,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})
in
\mathrm{L}^{r+1}((s,s+T);\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1})
for every
T>0
.

(iv)

\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\cdot,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{m,0})\to\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,s;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{0})
in
\mathrm{L}^{2}((s,s+T);\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{l}))
for every
T>0
and
0<l\leq m
,
where
\mathcal{O}_{l}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|x|<l\}
.

Proof.

Proof is similar to Lemma 4.2,
[
38
]
, and hence we omit it here.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

If
\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)(\text{ for }m\in\mathbb{N})
, then there exists an element
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)
such that upto a subsequence

(6.44)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\text{ in }\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})\text{ as }m\to\infty.

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
and attractor is a subset of absorbing set, we have that there exists a sequence
\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\theta_{-j}\omega)
such that
\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}=\Phi_{m}(j,\theta_{-j}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}
and

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{j,m,0}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}=\|\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq\mathscr{L}^{*}(\theta_{-j}\omega),

which implies that there exists an element
\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
such that upto a subsequence

(6.45)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{j,m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\ \text{ in }\ \mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})\ \text{ as }\ m\to\infty.

From Lemma
6.6
, we get

(6.46)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}_{m}(j,\theta_{-j}\omega)\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{j,m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\Phi(j,\theta_{-j}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\ \text{ in }\ \mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}).

It follows from the property of weak lower-semicontinuity of norm and (
6.45
) that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\liminf_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq\mathscr{L}^{*}(\theta_{-j}\omega),

which implies that
\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\in\mathcal{K}_{\infty}(\theta_{-j}\omega)
. Since
\Phi
is asymptotically compact (Lemma 5.7,
[
40
]
), for a sequence
t_{n}\to\infty
as
n\to\infty
, the sequence
\{\Phi(t_{n},\theta_{-t_{n}}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{t_{n},0}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
has a convergent subsequence. Therefore, there exists an element
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)
such that

\lim_{n\to\infty}\Phi(t_{n},\theta_{-t_{n}}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{t_{n},0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{0}.

From the invariance property of the random attractors, we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\Phi_{m}(t_{n},\theta_{-t_{n}}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{t_{n},m,0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}

Hence, as
j\to\infty
, (
6.46
) gives

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\text{ in }\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}),

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

For
r>1
, if
\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)(m\in\mathbb{N})
, then there exists an element
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)
such that upto a subsequence

(6.47)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,0}\to\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\text{ strongly in }\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}).

Proof.

Since
\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
, by (
6.44
), there exists an element
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)
such that

(6.48)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\text{ in }\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}).

Since
\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)
, by invariance property of attractors, for all
j>0
(for our purpose, we take
j
sufficiently large so that Lemmas
6.2
-
6.5
can be used properly in further calculations), there exists
\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\theta_{-j}\omega)
and
\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\in\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\theta_{-j}\omega)
such that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{m,0}=\Phi_{m}(j,\theta_{-j}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0})

and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{u}_{0}=\Phi(j,\theta_{-j}\omega)\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}).

From (
6.10
), we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})=\boldsymbol{u}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0})-\textbf{g}_{m}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\omega),

and

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})=\boldsymbol{u}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0})-\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\omega),

where

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}-\textbf{g}_{m}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{-j}\omega)\ \ \text{ and }\ \ \boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}=\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}-\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{-j}\omega).

Moreover, from (
6.48
), we have

(6.49)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\ \text{ in }\ \mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}).

From the fact that attractor is a subset of absorbing set, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{u}_{j,m,0}\|_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq\mathscr{L}^{*}(\theta_{-j}\omega),

which implies that there exists an element
\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\in\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
(due to the uniqueness of weak limit we are taking element
\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}
) such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{j,m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{u}_{j,0}\text{ in }\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}).

Consequently, we get

(6.50)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{j,m,0}\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}\text{ in }\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}).

By (
6.50
) and Lemma
6.6
, we have

(6.51)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})&\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{2}(-j,0;\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})),\\
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})&\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{2}(-j,0;\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})),\\
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})&\xrightharpoonup{w}\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{r+1}(-j,0;\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})),\\
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})&\to\boldsymbol{v}(\cdot,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\text{ in }\mathrm{L}^{2}(-j,0;\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{l})),\end{aligned}}}\right.

for every
l>0
, where
\mathcal{O}_{l}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:|x|<l\}
. From (
6.13
) together with (
2.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\/\mathrm{d}\/}{\/\mathrm{d}\/t}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+2\alpha\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+2\mu\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+2\beta\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}

\displaystyle=2b_{m}(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y},\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y},\boldsymbol{v}_{m})+2\beta\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{m}(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}),\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}}}\right\rangle+2\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{m},\boldsymbol{v}_{m}}}\right)

(6.52)

\displaystyle\quad+2\boldsymbol{y}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\ell-\alpha}}\right)\textbf{g}_{m}-\mu\mathrm{A}\textbf{g}_{m},\boldsymbol{v}_{m}}}\right).

An application of variation of constant formula yields

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}

(6.53)

\displaystyle=I_{1}(m,j)+I_{2}(m,j)+I_{3}(m,j)+I_{4}(m,j)+I_{5}(m,j)+I_{6}(m,j)+I_{7}(m,j)+I_{8}(m,j),

where

\displaystyle I_{1}(m,j)

\displaystyle=e^{-\alpha j}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})},

\displaystyle I_{2}(m,j)

\displaystyle=-\alpha\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,

\displaystyle I_{3}(m,j)

\displaystyle=-2\mu\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,

\displaystyle I_{4}(m,j)

\displaystyle=-2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,

\displaystyle I_{5}(m,j)

\displaystyle=2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}b_{m}(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0}))\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,

\displaystyle I_{6}(m,j)

\displaystyle=2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{m}(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)),\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right\rangle\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,

\displaystyle I_{7}(m,j)

\displaystyle=2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{f}_{m},\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,

and

\displaystyle I_{8}(m,j)

\displaystyle=2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\big((\ell-\alpha)\textbf{g}_{m}-\mu\mathrm{A}_{m}\textbf{g}_{m},\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\big)\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi.

Similar to (
6.1
), by (
6.12
), it can also be obtained that

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}=e^{-\alpha j}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}-\alpha\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad-2\mu\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi-2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}b_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}))\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right\rangle\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

(6.54)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\big((\ell-\alpha)\textbf{g}-\mu\mathrm{A}\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\big)\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi.

Using the convergences obtained in (
6.51
), we have

(6.55)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} \limsup_{m\to\infty}I_{2}(m,j)&\leq-\alpha\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,\\
\limsup_{m\to\infty}I_{3}(m,j)&\leq-2\mu\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,\\
\limsup_{m\to\infty}I_{4}(m,j)&\leq-2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,\\
\lim_{m\to\infty}I_{7}(m,j)&=2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\boldsymbol{f},\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})}}\right)\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi,\\
\lim_{m\to\infty}I_{8}(m,j)&=2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\big((\ell-\alpha)\textbf{g}-\mu\mathrm{A}\textbf{g},\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\big)\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi.\end{aligned}}}\right.

For
I_{1}(m,j)
, from the definition of absorbing set, we have

(6.56)

\displaystyle I_{1}(m,j)\leq e^{-\alpha j}\mathscr{L}(\theta_{-j}\omega),

where
\mathscr{L}(\omega)
is the same as in (
6.2
). Finally, we find the convergence for
I_{5}(m,j)
and
I_{6}(m,j)
using the Lemmas
6.2
-
6.5
. For convenience, we write

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi):=\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\ \text{ and }\ \boldsymbol{v}(\xi):=\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}).

Now, choose some
k
sufficiently large so that Lemmas
6.4
and
6.5
can be used in further calculations and consider
m\geq k
. For
I_{5}(m,j)
, we consider

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|I_{5}(m,j)-2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}b_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}))\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big((\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega))\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\big((\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega))\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big((\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega))\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big((\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega))\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big((\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi))\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big(\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi))\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big(\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi))\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big(\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big(\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

(6.57)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{2}\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\big(\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\nabla\big)\textbf{g}(x)\cdot\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)\big|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi=:\sum_{i=1}^{7}I_{5i}(m,j,k).

Using Hölder’s inequality and (
2.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|I_{51}(m,j,k)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\|\nabla(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi))\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\ \ +C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\sup_{\xi\in[-j,0]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}}}\right]\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{2\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{4}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\sup_{\xi\in[-j,0]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}}}\right]\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}}\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{2\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{4}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

For sufficiently large
j>0
, making use of Lemmas
6.2
and
6.3
, and strong convergence in (
6.51
), we obtain

(6.58)

\displaystyle I_{51}(m,j,k)\to 0\ \text{ as }\ m\to\infty.

Similarly, we deduce

(6.59)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} I_{52}(m,j,k)&\to 0\ \text{ as }\ m\to\infty,\\
I_{53}(m,j,k)&\to 0\ \text{ as }\ m\to\infty.\end{aligned}}}\right.

Again, using Hölder’s inequality and (
2.3
), we get

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|I_{54}(m,j,k)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\nabla\textbf{g}(x)\big|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\nabla\textbf{g}(x)\big|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{\xi\in[-j,0]}\bigg[\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg]\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\nabla\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)\big|^{2}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Lemma
6.5
and
\textbf{g}\in\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{A})
imply that, for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exists some
j>0
and
k>0
such that

(6.60)

\displaystyle I_{54}(m,j,k)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}.

Similarly, with the help of Lemma
6.4
and
6.5
, we have

(6.61)

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{\begin{aligned} I_{55}(m,j,k)&\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4},\\
I_{56}(m,j,k)&\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4},\\
I_{57}(m,j,k)&\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}.\end{aligned}}}\right.

From (
6.1
)-(
6.61
), we find

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|I_{5}(m,j)-2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}b_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}))\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi}}\right|\leq\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon
is arbitrary, taking
\varepsilon\to 0
, we obtain

(6.62)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}I_{5}(m,j)=2\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}b_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega),\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0}))\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi.

For
I_{6}(m,j)
, we consider

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|I_{6}(m,j)-2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right\rangle\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta\bigg|\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\int_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big[|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r-1}\big(\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-|\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r-1}\big(\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\big)\big]\textbf{g}(x)\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}(x)}}\right|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\quad+2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}(x)}}\right|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

(6.63)

\displaystyle\quad=:I_{61}(m,j,k)+I_{62}(m,j,k)+I_{63}(m,j,k).

Using Taylor’s formula (Theorem 7.9.1,
[
18
]
), (
2.4
), Lemmas
6.2
-
6.3
and the final convergence in (
6.51
), we get

\displaystyle I_{61}(m,j,k)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big[|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r-1}+|\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r-1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}(x)}}\right|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r-1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r-1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\|\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{3-r}}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{\frac{2(r+1)}{3-r}}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg[\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\frac{4\alpha\xi}{3-r}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{\frac{2(r+1)}{3-r}}\bigg]^{\frac{3-r}{2(r+1)}}\bigg[\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{r-1}{r+1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{r-1}{r+1}}\bigg]\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(6.64)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ m\to\infty,

for
1<r<3
, and

\displaystyle I_{61}(m,j,k)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\int_{\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big[|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r-1}+|\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)|^{r-1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(x,\xi)}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\textbf{g}(x)}}\right|\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r-1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r-1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{r-1}}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{\frac{r-2}{r-1}}_{\mathbb{L}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\|\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2(r+1)}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2(r+1)}(\mathcal{O})}\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r-1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r-1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right]
