\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{\frac{r-2}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}+\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{\frac{r-2}{r-1}}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}}}\right]\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{\frac{1}{r-1}}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi

\displaystyle\leq C\|\textbf{g}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2(r+1)}(\mathcal{O})}\bigg[\int\limits_{-j}^{0}e^{\frac{2r\alpha\xi}{r-1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{2(r+1)}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2(r+1)}}\bigg[\bigg(\int\limits_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{r-1}{r+1}}

\displaystyle\ \ +\bigg(\int\limits_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{r-1}{r+1}}\bigg]\bigg[\bigg(\int\limits_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)+\textbf{g}_{m}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{r-2}{r^{2}-1}}

\displaystyle\ \ +\bigg(\int\limits_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\|\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)\|^{r+1}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}^{r+1}(\mathcal{O})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{r-2}{r^{2}-1}}\bigg]\bigg(\int\limits_{-j}^{0}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(\xi)-\boldsymbol{v}(\xi)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{k})}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2(r-1)}}

(6.65)

\displaystyle\to 0\ \text{ as }\ m\to\infty,

for
r\geq 3
. Applying Hölder’s inequality in
I_{62}(m,j,k)
, we have

\displaystyle I_{62}(m,j,k)

\displaystyle\leq 2\beta\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(x,\xi)+\textbf{g}(x)\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\zeta}\omega)|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{r}{r+1}}

(6.66)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\bigg(\int_{\mathcal{O}_{m}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{k}}\big|\nabla\textbf{g}(x)\big|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/x\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r+1}}\bigg(\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right|^{r+1}\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi\bigg)^{\frac{1}{r+1}}.

Lemma
6.5
and
\textbf{g}\in\mathbb{V}(\mathcal{O}_{\infty})\cap\mathbb{H}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{\infty})
imply that, for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exist some
j>0
and
k>0
such that

(6.67)

\displaystyle I_{62}(m,j,k)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

Similarly, with the help of Lemma
6.5
, we find

(6.68)

\displaystyle I_{63}(m,j,k)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

From (
6.1
)-(
6.68
), we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|I_{6}(m,j)-2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right\rangle\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi}}\right|\leq\varepsilon.

Since
\varepsilon
is arbitrary, taking
\varepsilon\to 0
, we deduce

(6.69)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}I_{6}(m,j)=2\beta\int_{-j}^{0}e^{\alpha\xi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\langle\mathcal{C}_{\infty}(\boldsymbol{v}(\xi,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})+\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)),\textbf{g}\boldsymbol{y}(\theta_{\xi}\omega)}}\right\rangle\/\mathrm{d}\/\xi.

Combining (
6.1
)-(
6.56
), (
6.62
) and (
6.69
), we arrive at

(6.70)

\displaystyle\limsup_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq e^{-\alpha j}\mathscr{L}(\theta_{-j}\omega)+\|\boldsymbol{v}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}.

Since
j
can be large enough, passing
j\to\infty
, we get

(6.71)

\displaystyle\limsup_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}\leq\|\boldsymbol{v}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}.

From (
6.49
), we have

(6.72)

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{v}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\leq\liminf_{m\to\infty}\|\boldsymbol{v}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}_{m})}.

Now, (
6.71
) and (
6.72
) give that

(6.73)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_{m}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,m,0})\to\boldsymbol{v}(0,-j;\omega,\boldsymbol{v}_{j,0})\text{ in }\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O}),

which implies (
6.47
), and it completes the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove the main result of this section. The following Theorem proves the upper semicontinuity of random attractors with respect to domain.

Theorem 6.9
.

For
r>1
, the sequence
\{\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)\}_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of random attractors associated with the system (
6.2
) satisfies

(6.74)

\displaystyle\lim_{m\to\infty}\emph{dist}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m}(\omega),\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)\big)=0,\ \text{ for all }\ \omega\in\Omega,

where
\emph{dist}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}_{m}(\omega),\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)\big)
=
\sup\limits_{\boldsymbol{u}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}}\emph{dist}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\big(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}},\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)\big)
is the Hausdorff semidistance of the space
\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

We prove (
6.74
) by contradiction. If (
6.74
) is not true, then there exists
\delta>0
and a sequence
\boldsymbol{u}_{m}\in\mathcal{A}_{m}(\omega)
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) such that

(6.75)

\displaystyle\text{dist}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\big(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m},\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)\big)\geq\delta,\ \ m\in\mathbb{N}.

Using Lemma
6.8
, we can find a subsequence
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{m_{k}}\}
of
\{\boldsymbol{u}_{m}\}
such that

\text{dist}_{\mathbb{H}(\mathcal{O})}\big(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{u}}_{m_{k}},\mathcal{A}_{\infty}(\omega)\big)=0,

which is a contradiction to (
6.75
) and it completes the proof.
∎

Remark 6.10
.

In this section, we prove upper semicontinuity of random attractors with respect to domain for
r>1
only. For
r=1
, we need an additional assumption on
\textbf{g}(\cdot)
(see Assumption 1.1,
[
40
]
). Moreover, upper semicontinuity with respect to domain for 2D stochastic NSE (that is,
\alpha=0
and
\beta=0
) as well as 2D SCBF equations (for
r=1
) can also be proved in similar way with a few changes in calculations.
